# Anemone comparison matrix

This full matrix compares Anemone, GNmac, Morrow and Curtis, retaining all component,
benchmark, profile and dated comparison rows. Measurements from different source,
hardware, precision or workload epochs remain separate. Original weights use native
RTX PRO arithmetic, so results depend on assigned layers. Exact is limited to one
session and 512 total tokens.

Current runtime evidence uses engine epoch `1b0731a` and executable SHA256
`acdf429da22ae7a39504b897106e8cd56a22c3661bf086f60b5458ba5313f2af`.
There are 42 attempts in the 86-job non-golden catalogue: 40 passes, one TP4 mixed
identity failure and one unqualified capacity cleanup attempt; 44 jobs remain unattempted.
The default TP4 mixed run differs in 37/96 comparisons, with its rates withheld.
PP5 mixed and the separate TP4 prompt-chunk guard each pass 96/96. The guard has
no independent full-logit golden score. Current full golden GPU replay and Pi
remain unrun; historical scores are explicitly dated.

Six Curtis jobs, four functional snapshot jobs, two 100K-class pilots and two Fraser
reasoning jobs pass their bounded protocols. Snapshot timing/rate claims are withheld;
the 100K pilots establish first-token execution and selected-record repeatability,
and Fraser's short diagnostics do not establish full-output or quality equivalence.
Capacity attempt 57 remains unqualified because its original server wait is missing;
later process absence does not repair that evidence. Attempts 58/60 did not start.

See [benchmark methods and source notes](BENCHMARKS.md), the [result summary](RESULTS_6NOV.json),
and [golden metrics](GOLDEN_METRICS.md).
The 297 implementation reviews are source findings, not GPU passes. Remaining
runtime work, fixture gates and unsupported or unimplemented capabilities remain explicit.
HumanEval/GSM8K acquisition and evaluation are outside this measured campaign.

## Measured source epochs

The dated donor/recipe epoch `6b9e276` has executable SHA256
`da39965f79adf7883f92c8941e2b92d928706dc7b3f55671b89dce23b67c413e`.
Its donor suites have 423 response identities per balanced format. Fourteen of its
16 recipe/cache/pause jobs passed; default TP4 warm/pause identity failures retain
withheld rates. These values are not current `1b0731a` reruns. Short donor TTFT
permits prefix reuse; slot-allocation readiness does not prove full-context quality.

Newer than our deployed versions: **GNmac none** (1.2.0 is the newest tag and commit). **Morrow 1.0.2** (2 November) only
fixes start-up on hosts without PCIe peer access (copy-engine ring built only with peer access) and updates notices;
speed and quality unchanged. The plugin switches added between our Mac clone (9ad6821) and HEAD 249b45b (split-KV decode
attention default off, compressed all-reduce v2 + one-shot, wo_a decode-once at 17-64 rows, FAST_LOAD, expert rotation,
unchunked prefill MoE, MoE ring kernel + drafter v2) were already inside the measured 1.0.1 image (plugin 0b34823c39).
**Curtis 2026.11.02-speed-k5** is new (2 November): first Docker release; lanes speed / bf16 / quality; BF16 KV
everywhere; GNmac's FP8 dense / wo_a / draft head / fused Markov adopted; fixed-5 verification, gate off.

older-boot number of the corresponding recipe family. (2) Anemone's 698 s readiness includes loading ~49 GiB of EXL3
experts a card from the pack with digests; GNmac/Curtis/Morrow load safetensors through vLLM.

(2) Curtis's controlled cache-only fixture measured PPL 1.16859 with BF16 KV against 1.18368 with FP8 KV (CB "Quality"),
the strongest public evidence that GNmac's fp8_ds_mla KV is lossy; Anemone's FP16 state keeps more mantissa than either.

Notes E: (1) On this box every card has its own bridge, so Curtis's socket-pair "hybrid P2P" has no direct analogue;
his P2P4 kit measured on reference machine chose FOURWAY_P2P/PyNCCL and was slower than his STEER_3 host recipe in every cell (CP).
(2) GNmac's C8 all-reduce is 10.9 ms of a 32.8 ms step, exposed; Anemone's BATCH rings were 19.3 ms of 77.9 ms before
tcb09 (one ring a step). The lossless compressed wire and round-once are the two collective ideas Anemone lacks.

Historical notes G (4 November comparison): Anemone's attention is at parity at C1 and the gap is prefill: Morrow computes all 40 layers on every row at ~6.1
us a row-layer against Anemone's ~25.8 us (GA "Per row and layer"); since then Anemone moved to 9.430 s on the 30K prompt
(rc02ab, exact default since 32a1299; rm01 12.0 s) against Morrow's 7.87 s (profiled). Curtis's integer-preparation
fusion (one Triton pass `_ragged` computing exact int64 prefix counts for ratio 1/2/window layers, ALIGNMENT 128) is the
cleanest idea for Anemone's per-slab prepare/query/index launches.

Notes M: Anemone's keyed sampler (sampler_records.h) makes every token a deterministic function of logits, seed and
position, independent of the batch and of the drafts whenever the logits themselves are (exact recipe, THIN; the relaxed
BF16 BATCH arithmetic changes logits with batch position, bsmoke02/probe03); Curtis reports "exact seeded output identity failed" for his
stack, and vLLM's rejection sampler consumes RNG per proposal. This is a precision/reproducibility point where Anemone
leads all three and should be stated in the release notes.

## N. Sampling and output numerics

Historical notes O (4 November comparison; current exact admission is C1 / capacity 512): (1) For the "better precision / lower lossy" goal Anemone already leads on storage precision (FP16 state and
FP16 index keys against BF16 / FP8; exact FP8 weight decodes like everyone), on reproducibility (keyed sampler, hash-
stable T=0) with a line-referenced profile; official-reference bit identity is still unqualified (4 Nov O/R, V10). What
it lacks is a **published common metric**:
run Curtis's public teacher fixture (96 requests, 6 investigation states, 0.4K / 7.3K histories) and GNmac's quality.py on
Anemone exact and relaxed, so the precision lead is a number. (2) Curtis's cache-only fixture (BF16 1.16859 vs FP8
1.18368) is the only cross-engine KV-precision measurement and it favors every non-GNmac engine.

| S11 | Authentication | Reviewed source semantics: key_file loads a regular nonempty file with no group/other permission bits; the documented setup creates0600, but source does not enforce an owner UID or exact 0600 bits. Named-user lists are validated (one legacy key line remains compatible). Keygen obtains32 random bytes through Linux getrandom(...,0), retries partial/EINTR, returns errors and zeros its temporary buffer. Disposable CLI/native-parser checks passed; no real key/config was read and crypto strength was not benchmarked. Remaining gate: Pi schema/keygen checks are bounded setup evidence, not a Pi end-to-end/security proof. Remaining gate: No new runtime claim. Existing executed host witnesses establish only their stated synthetic/parser scope. ## AB. Benchmark protocols (added: what each test measures)

| ID | Protocol | Prompts / work | Rate definition | Concurrency / repeats | Power / context | Engines with numbers here | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AB1 | MiaAI donor bench (GNmac bench/mia.py, blob d9b24cf, client 6278ecb) | structured = count 1-200, code = Python `clamp_range` with docstring (~95 tokens), prose = hash-map explanation; T=0, top_p 1, thinking off, 400-token cap | C1: median of 5 streaming rates after the first token; C2-C8: completion tokens / batch wall (TTFT included); table = median of 5 pass medians | 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 users | 180 W; Anemone 65,536 x 8 slots, GNmac / Morrow 262,144 | Anemone, GNmac, Morrow, Curtis k5 (3 lanes) | gnmac_tp4_v1 REPORT; G11; M11; W:gsa01 |
| AB2 | Morrow release protocol | MiaAI-Lab prompt sets (structured + code from the first set, prose from the second, each set on its own boot); cold prefill 2,048 and 32,768 tokens | completion tokens over the whole run including TTFT, median of 5 a boot, averaged over 2 boots | 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 | 180 W, 262,144, Engram in RAM | Morrow (SR); Curtis k5 here with the DecodeBench prompts (curtis_bench.py) | MRS |
| AB3 | Curtis "Morrow" decode (frozen hash bad399f0) | count / binary-search code / hash-map prose, 400 tokens, T=0, cache-zero | per-user (tokens - 1) / (last - first arrival), median of 3 batches; wall = whole-batch send-to-close incl. TTFT | users 1-8 | 140 W, 262,144 | Curtis (SR); Curtis k5 measured here (3 lanes, 180 W, DecodeBench reconstruction); older Curtis builds (CP) | CB "Final Morrow"; CP |
| AB4 | Curtis SPARK T=1 | 8 categories + counting ceiling; target T=1 | mean of 2 pass means of category full-wall aggregates; per-user (tokens - 1) / (close - first) | users 1 / 2 / 8 | 140 W | Curtis (SR); Curtis k5 here (3 lanes, 180 W; speed also with greedy proposals, k5z; speed at 140 W with and without greedy, k5e140) | CB "Bounded SPARK" |
| AB5 | Curtis cold prefill | one frozen cache-zero request a size (2K, 8K, 32K, 128K), one generated token | input tokens / request wall | 1 | 140 W | Curtis (SR); Curtis k5 here (3 lanes at 180 W; 128K speed (k5e140 180 W tail), bf16 (k5w2), quality (k5z); speed at 140 W, k5e140) | CB "Cold prefill" |
| AB6 | Curtis teacher fixture | 96 teacher-forced requests, 6 synthetic investigation states, ~0.4K / 7.3K histories, 138 gold tokens a pass | PPL = exp(mean NLL); critical-token NLL; 48 correct-vs-incorrect contrasts | 2 passes a configuration | n/a | Curtis lanes (SR); here his decode-graph probe for every lane (bf16 1 pass k5w2; speed / quality 2 passes k5z, pass 2 on a warm prefix cache; speed at 140 W 2 passes k5e140) and the API method (prefill logits, 1 pass) for speed / quality in k5x; fixtures public in benchmarks/2026-11-01/fixtures | CB "Quality" |
| AB7 | Fraser (gnmac_port fraser_collect.py over the frozen harness) | fixed ~2.7K-token code prompt (2,644 tokens Anemone), fresh nonce + cache salt, 2,048-token cap, T=0, thinking off | S = N / (last - first) a stream (N, not N - 1); aggregate = total / (latest end - earliest start); TTFT median | C1 10 reps, C4 / C8 3 batches | 180 W | Anemone, GNmac, Morrow, Curtis k5 (3 lanes) | GA lines 495-528; scripts/fraser_collect.py |
| AB8 | Cold prefill median of 9 (GNmac bench/prefill.py) | 3 public-domain texts (~24K, ~34K, ~38K tokens), 3 runs each, no cache hits | prompt tokens / TTFT | 1 | 180 W | Anemone, GNmac, Morrow, Curtis k5 | GS; W:*/prefill.log |

5 November Anemone protocol addendum: AB1 uses three pass medians, with the shipped legacy prefix_reuse1 and actual saved cache counters; fresh short TTFT is reuse-enabled, not cold. C1 has five requests per pass; C2/C4/C8 have three batches per pass. Native acceptance/proposed-depth fields use per-request command-source counters. AB5 grid keeps the four-slot131584 context protocol with a legal <=4-verifier BATCH shape menu (no supplied timing prices); three repetitions at2K/8K/32K, two at128K, actual cached0 required. AB7 retains10/3/3 nonce batches; stream count, finish, native usage/hash and cache receipts are read. AB8 retains all nine public-book requests, prefix_reuse0 and actual cached0. AB11 repeats each default generate prompt twice and compares complete selected8/256 records to separately pinned EXL3/original controls; canonical mc_check2 also covers2048/1024. AB9 and all dropped quality/trace/cache/snapshot/pause scopes were not rerun on5Nov. Original PP5 uses native PRO arithmetic† and merged omitted pipeline depth5 for multi-session serving. The original competitor protocol descriptions and measurements above are preserved.

Notes AA/AB: the "Morrow benchs" the owner names are two different things: Morrow's own release protocol (AB2) and
Curtis's frozen "Morrow" decode harness (AB3). Our 1:1 table (AB1) uses MiaAI prompts like AB2 but a different rate
definition at C1 (V5 derives AB2's C1 from AB1). No engine but Curtis has AB3 numbers yet; the main session's k5 run
includes it (PROMPT.md step 3).

## V. Benchmarks (results)

Current balanced donor results below are measured on 6 November, source 6b9e276 / spdx_02. The ten admitted historical recipe arms have also been rerun on that binary, with their explicit settings retained. Their numerical scope is T0 text/count/finish identity, not official full-state equivalence. The old 4 November gxp20/gxp26/THIN narratives are historical, including their old use of the phrase “exact default”; see the [complete prior matrix](COMPARISON_MATRIX.md). Other protocol groups remain separately pending.

The runs and pending states in this section are historical through 4 November. “Current-default” below means the tcb74 recipe at that time, not the current published profile. They are distinct from the 6 November functional HTTP smokes and establish no new Pi task-quality result.

4 November pi additions (metrics and receipts only; no blind repository, work directory or archive inspected):
v2 run 2 = adaptive, v2 run 3 = adaptive with measured C1 costs on rndl_14; v3 candidate 1 = current-default THIN fixed
5/policy 0 on tcb74. Main21:52: one 1M slot, wide 2048/rows 8, no BATCH/cost menu; candidate 3 changes only policy 2,
learned acceptance1 and14300/0/690affine costs, not N mode 11.
The v2 run 1 stopped after five requests and is not a completed fixed-5 comparison. The JSON `depth` field labels
the candidate, not speculative depth. v3 candidate 1's receipt is dated 21:27:19 CET (status 0 / 0); candidate 3 is
queued and **pending**. Rates above distinguish per-request median from timed-token aggregate. These are separate
T=1 trajectories, with legacy cache reuse; no T=0 identity or task-quality conclusion follows from these metrics.
No newer wall-time value was inferred from polling counts; the old wall row remains historical v1.

Curtis pi (k5pi): speed lane only, his 262,144-token context (his code refuses a 1M slot) and 1.5 GiB pool (294,137 KV
tokens), 2,640 s budget (the job's time left under gpu_run's ceiling). With pi's 65,536 max_tokens, prompts above
196,608 tokens were rejected (4 times); prompts averaged 106K tokens against 351K (GNmac), 399K (Anemone) and 511K
(Morrow), so his decode rate is measured at a shallower context (+79.9% over Morrow's 85.31 at 5x less context), and each
engine ran one T=1 trajectory. CURTIS_K5_REPORT.md 4.8.

The owner asked for the rerun at 1M (3 Nov), and static analysis came first (CURTIS_K5_REPORT.md 4.8a). With his
262,144 check opened, one 1M request needs a pool of 3,774,459,904 B (BF16) or 2,549,184,512 B (FP8): +2.02 / +0.87
GiB a card. vLLM's indexer prefill workspace (40 x context x 132 B) grows from 1.29 to 5.16 GiB a rank, against
1.97-2.44 GB free a rank at his ABC pre-check. So BF16 and FP8 do not fit as shipped (measured check of the workspace
term: k5i -> k5m profile growth +723 MiB vs +675 predicted). Owner: "Best 1M try, else 262K". Job k5pi2 (20:02-20:19) ran attempt C (1M, FP8 KV, memory-only caps).
C passed vLLM: GPU KV cache 1,049,657 tokens at 1.00x, workspace 1,057 MB, graphs 1.48 GiB. His attention bank and R2
qualified at 1M, and C stopped at his ABC capture pre-check: free 1,174,601,728 / 1,164,115,968 / 1,250,099,200 /
943,456,256 B on ranks 0-3, against 1,140,850,688 needed (rank 3, the desktop card, 197 MB short; exit 76). The
262,144 fallback was stopped during its boot on the owner's instruction ("curtis run is over, no need re-run"). The
Curtis pi result stays k5pi.
Morrow pi (attempt 4, 3 Nov 15:37-16:37, Morrow 1.0.1 recipe start.sh at `--max-model-len 1048576`, KV pool 1,287,027
tokens, 180 W): 85.31 tok/s decode over 198 requests (0 failed, 0 preemptions), 7 drafts verified a step at 33.9%
acceptance (3.37 tokens a step), prefix-cache hits 99.3%, mean TTFT 3.14 s, mean prompt 510,683 tokens (Anemone 398,971,
GNmac 350,893, Curtis 106,005); pi ran its whole 3,258 s budget (status 124, not finished). pi_ext_v1/metrics_morrow.json.

<a id="v10-original-weights-tp4_pro-vs-pp5-official-deepseek-reference"></a>

### V10. Historical original-weight development and reference tests (4 November)

All development, default and merge states below refer to 4 November. In particular, “branch, not merged” and “main remains 7d86c45” are historical statements. Current availability and profile dispatch are reviewed in the architecture source cells; strict exact admission is original PP5, one session and capacity 512, with its stated source-fixture limits. Historical microbenchmarks and failed official-reference gates are not new measurements or current defaults.

4 November 2026, track O: reference-order-1 original-weight baseline 936517b, native PRO, exact numerics,
lossless/raw TP, fixed DSpark 5/policy 0, pipeline depth 2, wide/full 1,024, rows 8, graphs 1, cone 1, one chunk in
flight,
T=0/top_p 1, reasoning 0; eight 64K serving slots; CMP 180 W, PRO 400 W unchanged. TP4_PRO = four CMPs together at
layers
0-28, PRO layers29-39 + head + DSpark (backend order 0, 1, 3, 4, 2). PP5 = CMP layer ranges0-7/8-14/15-21/22-28 and the
same PRO tail. This is not TP2_PP2_PRO; TP5 is unsupported. TP flights serialize at this baseline. No original-weight
BATCH support is claimed. All ports below are **branch, not merged**; main remains7d86c45.

Track R's explicit source call shape is part of its target: serial one-row execution cannot silently stand in for an
eight-row prompt. `--official_hc_rows` currently admits original thin PP only (precision/reference/CMP-reference,
no TP/wide/draft/vision/EXL3); it does not switch prompt8 to decode1 automatically. BF16 KV/RoPE boundaries and two
FP8 round trips are also missing from the ordinary path; independent candidates are built, device/runtime gates
pending for runtime/full-model (XR03 completed with overall failure; XR04 pending). One WQB projection value still
differs in the source-input CPU replays. Matching argmax 8/8
does not establish generation identity. Neither original placement has an official-exact full-model result.

O's `--original_wide_experts`, `--tp_pipeline_overlap`, `--original_batch`, `--original_chunk_core` and
`--original_queries_piece` are implemented / host-tested, default 0, **branch, not merged** (RND_O 21:59).
Their GPU bits/speed gates remain pending; none supplies a qualified new performance cell. PP5 depth 2/5/8 sweep, repeat
cold measurements after new ports, longer/mixed/rollback
state preservation, broad templates (12/326 mismatches), original BATCH and qualified R port remain open. Tokenizer
6329 texts/2413740IDs had zero differences (O FINAL_1,4Nov). No EXL3 donor score is substituted for these original
placements; O's gsl11m EXL3 context is a different numerical/program recipe.

Historical notes V (4 November comparisons, except the explicit current V1 pointer): (1) Anemone's C1 TTFT lead on short prompts is real (own server, no Python front end) but irrelevant to long
prompts, where its prompt rate rules (Fraser, cold prefill). (2) The new C2-C8 windows use explicit adaptive or
fixed-depth recipes, not one universal preset; draft history
and identical-prompt lockstep affect their rates (4 Nov RND_L / RND_N). The gxp20 control is historical (4 November); current balanced donor data are in V1. (3)
GNmac's
1:1 numbers here exceed its own SR in 11 of 12 cells (P2P on this box); Morrow's ours are below its SR because its SR
includes its own prompt set (code prompt 400 tokens) and formula.

It is superseded for source availability by the 297 reviewed dispositions above. Runtime-pending and unimplemented entries remain explicit; source review does not close their measured gates.
